# Ranked design decisions — research synthesis

Built from **63 YouTube videos** (full transcript + keyframe contact sheets) and **49 articles**. 2648 individual decisions were extracted, clustered by theme into 1064 distinct decisions, checked for over/under-merging and spot-checked against transcripts. Ranking = number of **independent creators** that state the decision (a channel with 4 videos counts once), then number of distinct videos/articles, then source quality. Categories = how many of the research sets (premium videos, Apple videos, anti-AI videos, rules videos, article angles) it appears in.

Notes: other: dropped 0 over-merged members, 0 merges, 4 rewrites, grounding 14/14 supported; accessibility_performance: dropped 3 over-merged members, 2 merges, 6 rewrites, grounding 10/12 supported (unsupported: GFTDR8_q63M#10 7PeSluyP8FE#10); brand_identity: dropped 0 over-merged members, 3 merges, 7 rewrites, grounding 17/17 supported; copy_content: dropped 7 over-merged members, 1 merges, 10 rewrites, grounding 27/30 supported (unsupported: 8pMUkEbAM7g#14 7PeSluyP8FE#13 r8QkBKpSj0o#16); imagery_media: dropped 5 over-merged members, 2 merges, 10 rewrites, grounding 25/25 supported; ai_tells: dropped 14 over-merged members, 4 merges, 9 rewrites, grounding 20/20 supported; hierarchy: dropped 12 over-merged members, 4 merges, 13 rewrites, grounding 21/23 supported (unsupported: -mlAdcQZZAM#6 jOTYHGfxrl0#3); components_ui: dropped 3 over-merged members, 2 merges, 7 rewrites, grounding 22/23 supported (unsupported: vvPklRN0Tco#23); spacing_layout: dropped 4 over-merged members, 5 merges, 8 rewrites, grounding 24/24 supported; motion_interaction: dropped 8 over-merged members, 4 merges, 8 rewrites, grounding 22/22 supported; color: dropped 8 over-merged members, 4 merges, 9 rewrites, grounding 21/21 supported; typography: dropped 7 over-merged members, 10 merges, 11 rewrites, grounding 24/25 supported (unsupported: 7PeSluyP8FE#15); process_mindset: dropped 18 over-merged members, 3 merges, 3 rewrites, grounding 19/19 supported; apple: dropped 12 over-merged members, 5 merges, 12 rewrites, grounding 23/23 supported

| 1 | ✅ Use far more whitespace than feels necessary. Roughly double your default margins and gaps (2-3x a standard interface for premium or editorial layouts) and add extra vertical space on mobile. | 2 | ✅ Set every gap by relationship, never one uniform value. Keep related items tight (4-16px, with labels 4-8px from their input) and separate groups by at least 2x the inner gap (24-80px). ## Things that make design look AI-generated (ranked)

| # | Decision | Theme | Creators / sources (videos/articles) | Research sets | Specifics |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⛔ Don't assemble the page from a template or from your category's stock section sequence. For SaaS that sequence is: announcement bar, pill badge, gradient hero with product mock, logo cloud, stat strip, three identical feature cards, a '3 simple steps' stepper, testimonial cards, three-tier pricing with a highlighted middle, CTA strip, 4-column footer and floating chat button. Decide the layout and information architecture yourself from this page's content, and give sections their own moments of interest instead of repeating one component pattern. | ai_tells | **14** / 14 (7v/7a) | 7 | Stock sequences appear in NRE4's BEFORE page, cHb4's SpectraAI page, pY52's 'Welcome To Our Website!', pg-37's prototype and GFTDR8's $500 Wix tier ('they grab a template, they swap the text, the images, they add a few c |
| 2 | ⛔ Don't default to Inter, Roboto, Arial, Open Sans, Lato, Times New Roman or the system/browser/design-tool default font as the brand's voice. Set headings in a face with character (a distinctive sans, a serif or a proprietary face) and keep display and body voices distinct. A neutral workhorse face is acceptable for body or UI text beneath it. | ai_tells | **13** / 13 (4v/9a) | 6 | Near-universal ban list: the Anthropic prompt (a00), NRE4's on-screen prompt, a05, a06, a08 and a13 (which adds Times New Roman). io31: 'nine times out of 10' the 'looks AI' feeling is just default type. a10: brands warn |
| 3 | ⛔ Don't default to purple/violet (hue about 250-300 degrees) as the brand colour. That means no purple-to-blue or purple-to-magenta gradients on hero backgrounds, headline text, preview frames or buttons (worst on a white page), and no indigo/violet solid fill on CTAs, active pills or badges. ## Apple playbook (ranked by Apple-related sources)

| # | What Apple does | Creators / sources | Specifics |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Show products as large, isolated, centred cutouts on a seamless white or very light grey field (#F5F5F7; black for Pro), in a clean three-quarter or frontal view with only a soft contact shadow and generous empty space. Shoot in a spare, neutral, analytical style with no props or lifestyle clutter. | **9** / 9 | Apple Watch trio on white, AirPods Pro on light grey, iPhone on black. Optional slow 3D camera moves or graphic arrangements (rows of colourways, radial fans). Zuckerman's deliberately spare style. |
| 2 | Set all type from a fixed set of named semantic text styles that lock size, weight and leading and scale together. Never hardcode sizes or eyeball custom styles. Use about 3 styles per component, e.g. title, then subheadline in the secondary colour, then body. | **7** / 8 | iOS: Large Title 34/41, Title 1 28/34, Title 2 22/28, Title 3 20/25, Headline 17/22 Semibold, Body 17/22, Callout 16/21, Subhead 15/20, Footnote 13/18, Caption 1 12/16, Caption 2 11/13. Replace Font.system(size: 17) with .font(.body), and .body plus bold with  |
| 3 | Set the whole interface in one sans family (SF Pro or system-ui) and build hierarchy from size and weight alone: bold for large headlines, regular or medium for subheads and body. Keep typefaces to a minimum, and never add a decorative or second display face. | **5** / 5 | font-family: 'SF Pro', system-ui, -apple-system, sans-serif. A white background plus SF Pro alone 'already makes it look like Apple'. New York is the only sanctioned serif companion. |
| 4 | Keep essential actions and information visible and reachable in one tap, and keep the most frequent tasks within about three taps. Put only nonessential actions in overflow (ellipsis) menus. Never reveal essential information or functions only on hover, because touch has no hover. When there are only one or two items, show them directly instead of behind a summary card with a 'See all' link. If it matters, show it; if it doesn't, cut it. | **5** / 5 | 'When we say simple, we don't mean minimal': burying functionality in one place looks minimal but isn't simple (Xa9L2yx_QH8 [11:20]). Hover-only reveals are an anti-pattern and hover doesn't exist on mobile (DNSXlBmukck [08:00]). iOS 26 Safari hid the tab swit |
| 5 | Make content the priority (Deference). Bars, controls and decoration should recede like a window onto the content: always available, never intrusive, never competing for attention or blocking content and touch targets. | **5** / 5 | Liquid Glass end state: 'Liquid, responsive, and almost invisible'. iOS 26 goal: 'bringing greater focus to content'. HIG principle #2, Deference. |
| 6 | Use exactly one accent colour, system blue (~#0066CC on the web, ~#007AFF on iOS), for every interactive element: links, Buy pills, controls, focus and selected states, plus at most key data such as prices and count badges. Keep everything else black, grey and white. Never use the accent decoratively, and never add a second accent. | **5** / 5 | Blue appears only on Buy pills and links, with thin line icons as the only graphics. Fifteen design files forbid a second accent. |
| 7 | Strip each screen to the fewest elements in the simplest arrangement. Remove every element, badge, banner and word the task doesn't need, then add back only what earns its place. Never strip by hiding functionality people need. | **5** / 5 | 'In a simple interface, every element earns its place.' 'Remove everything, then add back strategically.' 'Cheap design adds, expensive design subtracts.' Audit a screen by asking what is missing. |
| 8 | Commit to one design language (the same materials, patterns, controls and visual style) and apply it to every product, platform and page, from the smallest switch to full screens, so any single piece is instantly recognisable as yours. Each product should still work standalone and keep its platform-specific qualities. Don't reinvent the style every year. | **5** / 5 | Braun/Rams lineage of record players, calculators and clocks sharing one language. The iOS/iPadOS/macOS/watchOS/tvOS 26 design is the first to span every platform. Anti-example: apple.com changed its look three years running. |
| 9 | Use very few words. Let the product and its imagery carry the message. Keep on-screen text to a bare product wordmark or one short headline with at most a brief paragraph (~3-6 lines) per viewport, never blocks of explanatory copy. | **5** / 5 | About 72 film keyframes whose only text is a grey 'MacBook Pro' wordmark (xrhNIU-BLZw#17). The book has about 450 images and 'very few words' (a42#7, a43#0). 'Not too many words visible at one time on your screen' (KZ-eAiBW6gA#4). |
| 10 | Keep the page and base palette neutral (white, very light grey #F5F5F7, aluminium silver, space grey, black) and let product finishes, materials and on-screen artwork bring all the colour. At most, echo a product colour in one headline word. Never use loud or complementary combinations. | **5** / 5 | Fruit iMacs, Smart Covers in tan and light blue, the red iPhone 5c and a gold watch with a red band, all shown against neutral sweeps. |
| 11 | Default to the platform's native components and conventions so the app feels native and predictable and picks up new system materials automatically. That means the HIG, system frameworks, standard window chrome and navigation, and established placements such as search position and titles. Add custom personality only in deliberately chosen places. Never borrow another platform's style, and break a convention only when the benefit clearly outweighs the relearning cost. | **4** / 6 | Use SwiftUI glassEffect/Material, UIKit UIVisualEffectView and AppKit NSVisualEffectView before hand-building glass. Use Apple Design Resources templates. Anti-example: iOS 26 moved search to the bottom and switched to Android-style left-aligned titles. |
| 12 | Define every colour as a semantic role-named token (label, secondaryLabel, systemBackground, accent, divider, disabled), documented with its hex and usage, and give each one light, dark, increased-contrast and vibrancy variants. Reference tokens by name in every component spec (e.g. {colors.primary}) and never inline raw hex. | **4** / 5 | iOS greys: #E5E5EA dividers, #8E8E93 disabled, #3A3A3C secondary text, #1C1C1E dark surface. apple.com roles: Action Blue #0066cc, Parchment #f5f5f7, Ink #1d1d1f, Hairline #e0e0e0. #007AFF is community-measured and varies by OS. |
| 13 | Set running body text at the platform default and treat it as the floor for main reading text: 17pt on iOS and visionOS, 13pt on macOS, and 17px with 1.47 line-height on the web (not the conventional 16px). Never set readable text below Caption 2, which is 11pt on iOS and 10pt on macOS. | **4** / 4 | HIG defaults and minimums: iOS 17/11pt, macOS 13/10pt, tvOS 29/23pt, visionOS 17/12pt, watchOS 16/12pt (a33#0). apple.com body is SF Pro Text 17px/400/1.47/-0.374px (a10#22). Use .body for running text and nothing smaller than .caption2 (a39#19, a39#20). |
| 14 | Animate state and view changes instead of letting the UI snap or cut. Morph one shape into the next (an icon into its widget or card, an app into its icon, the Dynamic Island between states) and expand cards into detail views. Bring views in from, and back to, their spatial source (the tapped icon, the Island, the right edge for a pushed screen) so motion shows where content came from. | **4** / 4 | In After Effects, use path keyframes on a single shape layer (5j4HmfgPCDI#9). During the swipe-up morph the icon edge stretches to fill the frame (a19#14). Apps launch from their icon when it's visible and otherwise slide in from the right (a19#12). |
| 15 | Convey hierarchy and spatial relationships through depth layering. Stack translucent, background-blurred planes over the content and add parallax and motion, so a consistent system never feels fully flat. Keep individual icons and buttons flat, and pick one depth mechanism and use it consistently. | **4** / 4 | iOS 7 Notification Center as a translucent sheet. The Apple News floating glass tab bar. macOS Settings over wallpaper. iOS 26 'inspired by the depth and dimensionality of visionOS'. HIG Depth principle. |
| 16 | Write the design system down as a formal spec (HIG or DESIGN.md). Open with a short design-philosophy statement, then give exact tokens in sections for colour roles, typography, components, spacing and layout (including tap target and material) and accessibility. Close with explicit Do's and Don'ts that name the anti-patterns that would break the brand. Hold every component, interaction and AI generation prompt to the spec. | **4** / 4 | Apple II guidelines, then 'The Apple Desktop Interface', then today's HIG. The Apple DESIGN.md has 8 Do's and 8 Don'ts (a10#5). Section order: philosophy, palette, typography, components, layout, accessibility (pY52H5gKhGg#6). A sample prompt names SF Pro, 17p |
| 17 | Finish the smallest details nobody will consciously see (interiors, hidden states, alignment) to the same standard as the hero surface and primary function, even when it costs time and money. Hold every peripheral touchpoint to that standard too: sound, haptics, packaging, shipping box and printed matter. | **4** / 4 | Apple finished the iPod's interior despite the cost (xrhNIU-BLZw#1). People 'discern far more than they can articulate' (xrhNIU-BLZw#0). Every touchpoint (screen, sound, haptics, voice) gets the same craft (doiaMtD80p8#9). The shipping box has a draw tab and t |
| 18 | Use giant full-bleed photos or video as section building blocks, and overlay a short white, centred headline (plus at most one text link) directly on the image. | **4** / 4 | 2014 'Shot on iPhone 6': an edge-to-edge sky photo with a centred logo, headline and 'View the World Gallery >' (7PeSluyP8FE#11). AirPods Max: greyscale macro video of mesh with centred white headlines (cdzbM0HABqY#3). 'Try to find a more advanced mobile displ |
| 19 | Tie product video and animation to scroll position rather than autoplay, so the visitor controls the pace of the story: hardware reveal, then software demo, then use in context. Rotate or assemble the product to reveal a key attribute while headlines fade in and out. Nothing should move without narrative meaning. | **4** / 4 | AirPods Pro assembling on black. The iMac rotating to show its 11.5 mm profile. MacBooks folding open. 'The scroll isn't there to impress you. It's there to control the pace.' |
| 20 | Surround every element with drastic, confident white space, often letting a whole viewport hold one short line or one product shot. Take all spacing from a token scale. apple.com reference values, measured by a single source (a10): 80px section padding, at least 64px above headlines, 48-64px below, at least 40px around product images, 24px card padding. | **4** / 4 | Space tokens 4/8/12/17/24/32/48/80. Max width ~980px for text and ~1440px for grids (a10#32). 'Drastic amounts of white space' (cdzbM0HABqY [00:44]). 'Apple always leaves breathing room. Nothing feels cramped' (5j4HmfgPCDI [02:44]). |
| 21 | Switch Pro, flagship and rugged products to a pure black canvas with white type and low-key, rim-lit, cinematic renders (colour variants fanned on black, moody shots). Keep mainstream product pages on white or light grey. | **4** / 4 | Black: iPhone 11 Pro, AirPods Pro, 2007 iPhone launch, iPhone 7 jet black, Watch Ultra in extreme environments. Light: iPad, iMac, iPhone 11. |
| 22 | Write headlines and taglines as the product name plus a very short, confident, often witty declarative fragment or two-beat line about one benefit. End each sentence with a full stop, and often stack one to three words per line. | **4** / 4 | 'iMac. Internet expressway.', 'Pro. Go. Whoa.', 'Thinner. Lighter. Faster.', 'Light. Bright. Full of might.', 'Adventure awaits.', 'Looks tough. Because it is.' |
| 23 | Guarantee legibility of text and controls over glass and photos. Put a gradient or ~35% dark dimming layer behind them over bright or busy content, and make floating controls opaque enough to stand out, since blur alone isn't enough. Verify contrast against varied backgrounds before shipping. | **3** / 4 | Today's Pick hero gradient. 35% dimming applies only over bright content and is skipped where AVKit supplies its own. Maps icons stay lost in photos even with blur. The Liquid Glass beta shipped with contrast problems. |
| 24 | Put controls and navigation (tab bars, toolbars, sidebars, sticky sub-navs, the menu bar) on a distinct translucent material layer that floats above the content. Content scrolls under it and shows through, and stays the primary layer. Don't separate the layer with a shadow. | **3** / 4 | Liquid Glass (WWDC25) reflects and refracts its surroundings. The macOS Tahoe menu bar is fully transparent. apple.com sticky sub-navs are translucent. |
| 25 | Keep each key action and navigation control in the same place and state on every screen and device, and make controls that look alike behave alike. Keep learned gestures and placements through visual redesigns. Don't let controls appear, collapse or rearrange depending on context, and break an established pattern only deliberately. | **3** / 4 | The Mac window close button is always top-left. 'Design for muscle memory.' Anti-examples: identical buttons that navigate, toggle and open a modal; the Health tab bar collapsing on search; Safari's forward button appearing only in some states. |
| 26 | Build hierarchy with order, spacing, size and whitespace rather than colour, borders or effects, so the most important item on each screen is always the most obvious. Give each screen one visual anchor and check it with a squint test. | **3** / 4 | 'The most important item on the screen is always the most obvious one.' iOS 26 HIG Hierarchy principle. Let whitespace do the work so borders and boxes can recede. |
| 27 | In apps, use the system font through the platform font APIs and request a point size, so the OS applies the right optical size and per-size tracking automatically. Never embed SF, and never hand-adjust tracking or stroke per size in code. | **3** / 3 | SF Pro on iOS, iPadOS, macOS, tvOS and visionOS. SF Compact on watchOS. New York via Font.Design.serif. The opsz axis replaced separate SF Text and Display fonts. |
| 28 | Set tracking per size, never as one value. Use open (positive) tracking at caption sizes (11pt and below), zero at 12pt, and negative tracking from about 13pt to 22pt. At display sizes keep it near zero: the native SF table turns slightly positive and returns to 0 by 80pt, and apple.com headlines use only slight negative letter-spacing. Never tighten at 12px or below. | **3** / 3 | SF Pro tracking in 1/1000 em (a33#11): 6pt +41, 11pt +6, 12pt 0, 17pt -26 (-0.43pt), 20pt -23, 24pt +3, 28pt +14, 48pt +8, 80pt+ 0. apple.com: -0.374px at 17px body and -0.28px on the 56px/600 hero (a10#14). Display is tracked tighter than Text at equivalent o |
| 29 | Map weight to hierarchy: Regular for body and Semibold for same-size headings. iOS Headline is 17pt Semibold against Body at 17pt Regular. To separate same-size levels or add a level, switch to the next semantic style or apply an existing style's emphasized (bold) variant. Never add a new size, font or ad-hoc weight modifier. | **3** / 3 | Emphasized variants: Large Title, Title 1 and Title 2 become Bold; Title 3 and smaller become Semibold (SwiftUI .bold(), UIKit traitBold). Base title styles are Regular (a44#4). Prefer .headline over .fontWeight(.bold) on .body (a39#7). |
| 30 | Make interactive elements behave like physical objects. Add momentum on flicks, friction on drags and rubber-band bounce at scroll ends. | 230 | Echo your own canonical imagery: recreate the exact composition of a famous launch image to link new work to the brand's visual history. | **1** / 1 | The book's first image matches the 1998 iMac launch shot composition. |
| 231 | Keep an objective physical archive of past work and return to earlier solutions when stuck. | **1** / 1 | Apple began its archive about 8 years before the book and had to buy back many of its own products. |
| 232 | Work as one tightly integrated, cross-disciplinary team with a single goal. | **1** / 1 | Many members had worked together for 20+ years. |
| 233 | Make building something genuinely great for people the core motivation, not a sentimental afterthought. | **1** / 1 | Steve Jobs's founding motivation: contribution to culture and community. |
| 234 | Accent edges with a bright, precisely cut contrasting finish against a matte body. | **1** / 1 | iPhone 5/5s diamond-cut chamfers with no secondary polishing, then double anodised. |
| 235 | Give large surfaces a fine, uniform matte texture through controlled abrasive blasting. | **1** / 1 | Spherical 65-micron zirconia beads on extruded aluminium (2nd-gen iPod nano). |
| 236 | Avoid 'orange peel' unevenness on glossy surfaces. Aim for high distinctness of image (DOI) so reflections stay crisp. | **1** / 1 | Apple Watch zirconia back at 3nm surface roughness. A perfect mirror has perfect DOI. |
| 237 | Keep processes that leave an imperfect surface finish, such as die casting, to hidden internal components, never visible surfaces. | **1** / 1 | Die casting is used only internally. |
| 238 | Cut glare and smudging on screens with anti-reflective and oleophobic coatings. | **1** / 1 | AR thin-film stack on MacBook displays and the iPhone lens. PVD oleophobic coating on iPhone and iPad glass. |
| 239 | Choose materials for the real conditions of touch and use: sweat, skin contact, hygiene and scratching. | **1** / 1 | 316L steel on Apple Watch (sweat), 304 steel on the iPhone 4 bezel (scratch), silicone ear tips that resist microbes, TPSiV Bumpers. |
| 240 | Design for passive, silent cooling (convection or a chimney effect) instead of adding fans where possible. | **1** / 1 | Power Mac G4 Cube draws air in from below and vents it through the top. |
| 241 | Focus the lineup on a few great products organised as a simple matrix (e.g. 2x2: consumer/pro by desktop/portable), and make the site feature only those, so the site gets simpler as the line does. | **1** / 1 | Jobs: 'Focus your best resources on a few great products.' By 1999 the iMac, Power Mac G4, iBook and PowerBook filled the matrix, and the store showed a matching 4-product grid (7PeSluyP8FE#4, #6). Later cuts include iPod shuffle/nano/classic and AirPort. Anti |
| 242 | Run the global nav bar edge to edge across the full browser width as a flat rectangle, not as a rounded bar inset inside a fixed-width page. | **1** / 1 | 2011: a glossy rounded bar inset in a centred fixed-width layout. 2014: a flat dark-grey full-width rectangle with evenly spread white labels. |
| 243 | Build pages fully responsive from full-width elements (nav bar, hero photos) that resize with the window, with a layout optimised for mobile. | **1** / 1 | The 2014 flat redesign was Apple's first truly responsive site. |
| 244 | On category pages, give each model its own full-width row, alternating image-left/text-right with text-left/image-right. Each row carries name, tagline, price, Buy and Learn more, and the page ends with a 'Compare models' block. | **1** / 1 | iPad Air / iPad / iPad mini rows, followed by a 'Compare iPad models' panel. Hero devices bleed off the viewport edge. |
| 245 | Tell a 'story of value' by breaking the product into its physical components and explaining each one in turn, with its own isolated render and a short callout. | **1** / 1 | AirPods Max: canopy, stainless-steel frame, telescoping arms, anodized aluminium cups, mesh cushions, Digital Crown. |
| 246 | Open a product page with a short 'marketing moment', a smooth intro animation that shows off the product before any content or price. | **1** / 1 | The AirPods Max page starts blank white. The giant wordmark appears, then the headphones animate down in front of it. Apple's attitude: 'Slow down. Let's just give you an experience.' |
| 247 | Set display words in a heavy weight with tight negative letter-spacing, near-black on light grey, with 1-3 words centred per frame. | **1** / 1 | Seen in the video's own frames: 'Spacing', 'Animations', 'word by word'. |
| 248 | Structure editorial sections with a small eyebrow label, a huge left-aligned 2-3 line headline with tight leading, and a ~6-line body paragraph offset below it, starting at the page midpoint. | **1** / 1 | 'Design' eyebrow, then 'A radically original composition.', then offset body copy (AirPods Max). |
| 249 | Use few photographs, make each one extremely high quality, and use only your own renders or commissioned shoots. Never use generic stock photography. | **1** / 1 | Studio-lit AirPods Pro renders, large iPhone camera close-ups. 'Apple websites never use generic stock photography, they are a turn off.' |
| 250 | Keep drop shadows very soft, diffuse and low-opacity under text, cards and products. | **1** / 1 | Soft shadows under the tilted Apple Card and under bold type. Tension: the 2014 flat redesign removed shadows from UI chrome entirely. |
| 251 | In Apple-style motion graphics, use bevel and emboss sparingly to give text or logos a slight dimensional finish. | **1** / 1 | After Effects Layer Styles > Bevel and Emboss. Low-confidence single source, and it sits in tension with Apple's flat UI chrome. |
| 252 | Present a product's sizes and finishes in a grid of dark rounded-corner tiles with gutters, mixing close-up product crops with one text tile that carries an outlined pill CTA. | **1** / 1 | iPhone 11 Pro 'closer look. Two sizes. Four finishes.' with 'View the gallery >'. |
| 253 | Ease every keyframe and never use linear interpolation. Favour a strong ease-out, or an S-curve ease-in-out for moves between two states. | **1** / 1 | Graph-editor curves. The preset shown reads '0.00, 0.88, 0.04, 1.00' (a very strong ease-out). |
| 254 | Reveal headlines with a short, strongly eased slide-up from behind a rectangular mask, so the text appears from an invisible edge, with motion blur on. | **1** / 1 | Keyframe Position from an offset below the final spot, with the mask around the final text area. |
| 255 | Reveal sentences word by word with staggered per-word position (~100 px up) and opacity (0 to 100). In After Effects, use one text animator with Based On = Words, Shape = Ramp Up, Ease High -50, Ease Low 100, and keyframe the Offset. | **1** / 1 | Either split words onto separate layers and stagger the keyframes (#18), or use a single range selector (#19). |
| 256 | Parent grouped UI elements to a null controller so the whole cluster moves and scales as one. | **1** / 1 | After Effects nulls. Also parent the glass stroke to its main shape. |
| 257 | Build Apple-style motion graphics from familiar iOS components: calendar icon, toggles, volume pill, message bubbles, avatar chips, count-up numbers. | **1** / 1 | Shape-layer recreations shown throughout the tutorial. |
| 258 | Alternate static image sections with small, short motion or video moments, and place one right after any text-heavy stretch to restore interest. Never animate continuously. | **1** / 1 | iMac page rhythm: motion, static, static, movement, then a scroll animation. 'Just small little bits of movement, small pieces of video.' |
| 259 | Keep the main composition centred so the eye always knows where to look, and avoid off-balance layouts. | **1** / 1 | Centred heroes: Apple Card, Apple Watch, AirPods. 'Confusion kills attention.' |
| 260 | Centre a large product image and scatter short callout blocks around it at varied positions that still snap to the grid columns. | **1** / 1 | AirPods Max headband: two callouts stacked in the left column and one lower in the right. 'It almost feels like our text is sporadically placed... It's technically on a grid.' |
| 261 | Lay out image galleries as staggered, masonry-like grids of unequal widths and heights, with consistent and generous gutters, margins and padding. | **1** / 1 | AirPods Max: a wide lifestyle photo next to a narrower, vertically offset mesh close-up with a caption. |
| 262 | Don't let deadlines or a headcount-times-time plan decide the design. Budget for the uncertainty of creative work. | **1** / 1 | Ive: most manufactured objects are 'testament to carelessness' because schedule drove their decisions. In design, unlike other parts of the business, you can't predict the result from people and time. |
| 263 | Keep a written record of times the project seemed doomed and how the team got through them, and reread it during new periods of doubt. | **1** / 1 | Ive describes explicit 'declarations' (we were exactly here, the sky was falling) and replaying example after example of things working out. |
| 264 | Aim the work at the future, but study the history of tools and objects instead of dismissing it. | **1** / 1 | Ive calls ancient tools extraordinarily beautiful, with a rich 'biography'. |
| 265 | Purpose-build components and imagery for the specific job instead of assembling off-the-shelf kits and stock templates. | **1** / 1 | Apple claims there is not one screw it didn't make specifically for the job. Tension: when building apps for Apple platforms, the HIG says to prefer standard system components (see the 'standard components' cluster). |
| 266 | Keep behavior consistent, but let layout reshape for each device, window size and multitasking split instead of forcing identical layouts. | **1** / 1 | iPadOS now resembles macOS while staying customizable, and apps using the new materials reshape in resized windows. |
| 267 | On large screens, let users choose where navigation goes: a menu bar to maximize vertical content space, or a sidebar for structured browsing. | **1** / 1 | Example: Apple Music on iPadOS. |
| 268 | Use icons in toolbars and tab bars only where they add meaning. | **1** / 1 | iOS 26 calls this the 'conscious usage of icons' within an improved control structure. |
| 269 | Don't combine a centred title with left-aligned content and a stray control on its own row. Align the heading, header controls and content to one left edge. | **1** / 1 | Apple's 'Saved products' page had a centred H1 and a lone 'Edit' link. The redesign left-aligns the heading with Edit in the same row. |
| 270 | On product cards, put the name on the left and the price right-aligned in the title row, the description beneath, and the action below that. | **1** / 1 | 'iPad Mini 256GB' with '$1099', and 'Apple Watch Ultra' with '$999'. |
| 271 | Put a small circular count badge in the accent colour next to a section heading to show how many items it holds. | **1** / 1 | For example, 'Your saved products' followed by a tinted '2' circle. |
| 272 | Keep an explicit 'View' button on each product card even when clicking the image already opens the product. | **1** / 1 | The View button sits under the description. |
| 273 | Make secondary header actions such as 'Edit' a small white button with a light border, right-aligned in the heading row, not a lone text link. | **1** / 1 | From the saved-products redesign. |
| 274 | Merge a generic page title and a duplicate sub-label into one personal heading, for example 'Your saved products'. | **1** / 1 | 'Saved products' plus 'All saved products' became 'Your saved products'. |
| 275 | Before redesigning, place the original screen in the background with layout guides, keeping its frame size and global navigation. | **1** / 1 | Done in Penpot, keeping Apple's dark global nav. |
| 276 | Practise by critiquing and quickly redesigning real screens from top companies, comparing before and after side by side. | **1** / 1 | Even Apple ships weak UI, and this redesign took about 1.5 minutes. |
| 277 | To help users choose between models, use side-by-side columns: product image, colour dots, name, 'From $' price, a blue pill Buy button and 'Learn more', followed by aligned spec rows. | **1** / 1 | 'Which Apple Watch is right for you?' compares Series 8, SE and Ultra on case sizes and feature icons. |
| 278 | Use extreme macro crops of edges, ports, buttons and material joins that bleed off the frame to show craftsmanship up close. | **1** / 1 | Examples: the tapered MacBook Air edge, the USB-C port, the Watch crown and band lugs, and the iPhone 7 dual camera. |
| 279 | Show internal architecture with exploded views, where components float apart in parallel planes and then settle into the enclosure. | **1** / 1 | Examples: MacBook 12in layers descending into the unibody, the iPhone 6 stack and the Taptic Engine. |
| 280 | Explain engineering with thin, single-weight technical line overlays (grids, outlines, cross-sections, charts) drawn directly on the product image. | **1** / 1 | Examples: a blue plus-sign grid on the Magic Mouse, a fan diagram with a waveform, and an EarPods cross-section. |

### Apple product-page anatomy

# How Apple designs its product marketing pages (apple.com)

**Labels.** Numbers marked **reported** are stated by a source. Numbers marked **observed** were seen in video frames. Some observed values are my own pixel measurements on the 640×360 keyframes, so treat them as ±10% ratios, not exact CSS. Source ids are listed in the key at the end. Video evidence covers roughly 2014–2024 apple.com: KZ-eAiBW6gA is about 2020, 7PeSluyP8FE runs to 2019, r8QkBKpSj0o is about 2022–23, and cdzbM0HABqY is about 2024. Most hex and pixel values for the web come from a10 and a44, which are community-extracted from public CSS or measured. They are not published by Apple (a44#8).

---

## 1) Page anatomy, top to bottom

**Global nav.**
- It is a single slim bar running the full window width. Edge-to-edge has been the rule since the 2014 flat redesign; before that it was an inset, rounded glass bar [7PeSluyP8FE f050→f058/060].
- In 2020 it held 7 text categories (Mac, iPad, iPhone, Watch, TV, Music, Support) plus 3 icons (logo, search, bag), with no dropdowns (reported) [KZ-eAiBW6gA].
- Height is 44px, pure black (reported) [a10#37]. In the 2020 capture the bar is a dark translucent grey of about #333 (observed, KZ-eAiBW6gA f015).

**Category sub-nav (category landing pages only).**
- A row of thin line-icon product thumbnails with tiny labels: models, Compare, accessories, OS [KZ-eAiBW6gA f015/f017; 7PeSluyP8FE f061].
- Small orange "New" labels mark new items [7PeSluyP8FE].
- Under it there is sometimes one thin notice or promo strip, such as a blue Apple Card instalments strip or a shipping notice [KZ-eAiBW6gA; 7PeSluyP8FE f001].

**Sticky local nav (product pages).**
- Product name on the left. A few small text links (Overview, Tech Specs, "Why iPad") and a compact blue **Buy** pill on the right. It stays on screen for the whole scroll [cdzbM0HABqY; KZ-eAiBW6gA].
- It is translucent light on light pages and dark on black pages [KZ-eAiBW6gA].
- Reported spec: 52px tall, #f5f5f7 at 80% opacity, `backdrop-filter: saturate(180%) blur(20px)`, no shadow [a10#37].

**Hero, the "marketing moment."**
- The page opens with a short cinematic set piece *before* content or price. On AirPods Max the page loads blank white, then a near-full-width black wordmark appears and the headphones descend in front of it [cdzbM0HABqY f022→f023/f027].
- The narrator frames the intent as: users ask to get to the price, and Apple says "Slow down" [cdzbM0HABqY].
- The standard hero stack is: optional eyebrow ("New", "27-inch model"), product name, one-line tagline, "From $price" (often with a monthly price), blue Buy pill, then "Learn more >" [KZ-eAiBW6gA f015/f017; 7PeSluyP8FE f061].
- 2024 iMac variant: a colour lineup, then "Built for Apple Intelligence.", Buy pill, and "From $1299 or $108.25/mo. for 12 mo." [cdzbM0HABqY f003].
- Pro variant: a black page with "And then there was Pro.", a 5-line paragraph, a price line, and "Watch the keynote" / "Watch the film" links with play glyphs [KZ-eAiBW6gA f030].

**"Get the highlights." carousel.**
- Wide rounded cards with short copy ("New vibrant colors. Strikingly thin design.") and a play/pause control with dots [cdzbM0HABqY f003].

**Feature chapters.** This is the long body. Each chapter covers one benefit and changes format from the one before. Recurring formats:
- A full-bleed greyscale macro texture video (knit mesh, steel) with one centred white headline that changes as you scroll [cdzbM0HABqY f046–051].
- A scroll-driven product rotation in an almost empty white viewport, next to one tiny paragraph (iMac turning to its 11.5 mm profile) [cdzbM0HABqY f011/f014/f039].
- A stacked headline floating alone on white: "Only / 11.5 mm. / Now / that's / thin." [cdzbM0HABqY f012].
- An editorial block: small eyebrow ("Design"), a huge left-aligned three-line headline, and the body paragraph offset into the right half [cdzbM0HABqY f066].
- A build story: an isolated product splits into parts (canopy, frame, telescoping arms, cups, Digital Crown). Each part gets a small callout that opens with a bold keyword, placed left or right of the centred product but on the grid [cdzbM0HABqY f026/f053/f065].
- A staggered two-image grid: a wide lifestyle photo next to a narrower close-up that sits lower, with a caption under it [cdzbM0HABqY f067/f068].
- A bento grid of dark rounded tiles mixing product crops with one text tile ("closer look. / Two sizes. Four finishes…") that carries an outlined pill button [KZ-eAiBW6gA f031–033].
- Feature photos with headlines laid over them, and device screens filled with artwork [KZ-eAiBW6gA f040–050, f064–071].
- A spec chapter, such as "A12Z Bionic" [KZ-eAiBW6gA f067].

**Colours and finishes.**
- "Seven vibrant colors. You do orange." Swatch dots sit beside a coloured product profile in a split grid [cdzbM0HABqY f037/f038].

**Compare.**
- "Which Apple Watch is right for you?" uses three columns: image, swatch dots, name, "From $", Buy pill and "Learn more", then aligned spec rows [r8QkBKpSj0o f052].
- Category pages end with a "Compare iPad models" panel [KZ-eAiBW6gA f023].

**Footer.**
- Parchment #f5f5f7 (reported) [a10#21]. Footer link stacks use line-height 2.41 (reported) [a10#23].

**Other page types.**
- **Homepage (2020):** one hero, two sub-heroes for flagship products, about six smaller tiles, then the footer (reported) [KZ-eAiBW6gA].
- **Seasonal homepage:** one headline ("Wrap up your gift list.") over products arranged in radial fans on white [7PeSluyP8FE f001].
- **Category page:** one full-width row per model, alternating the image side and text side, ending in Compare [KZ-eAiBW6gA f020–023].

**Section rhythm.**
- Full-bleed bands switch between light, dark and parchment with 0px gaps, so the colour change itself divides sections. The same surface is never used twice in a row (reported) [a10#21].
- Layouts must be "structured" (on a grid) and "exploratory" (with moments that break the expected pattern). The same layout is not repeated section after section [cdzbM0HABqY].

## 2) Type system

**Family**
- SF Pro is used throughout. It switches between a **Text** optical cut below 20pt and a **Display** cut at 20pt and above (reported) [a38#0; a39#11].
- One family carries all hierarchy; the only sanctioned companion is the New York serif. Do not add a second display font [a33#5; a44#0–1].
- Web stack: `system-ui, -apple-system, BlinkMacSystemFont`. If falling back to Inter, use weight 600, tighten display tracking by −0.01em, and drop body line-height from 1.47 to 1.44 (reported) [a10#31].
- A motion tutorial says headings use SF Pro Display Bold and secondary text uses Regular/Medium (reported, not about apple.com) [5j4HmfgPCDI].
- History: condensed serif in the late 1990s, thin sans in 2011–14, heavy bold sans by 2019 (observed) [7PeSluyP8FE f030/f058/f060/f001].

**Weights**
- Web headlines are reported at 600. The weight ladder is 300/400/600/700, with 500 left out. 300 is used only for airy large text, such as a 24px/300 lead paragraph (reported) [a10#23].
- Pushing display text to 700 or more "reads as marketing-page-template" (reported) [a10#15].
- HIG: avoid Ultralight, Thin and Light for interface text [a33#2]. Extreme Display weights belong only at large sizes [a38#7].
- Observed: the "AirPods Max" wordmark is set very heavy and at about viewport width as a graphic [cdzbM0HABqY f015/f023].

**Sizes and line-height (web, reported [a10#14/#22/#23])**

| Style | Size / weight / line-height / tracking |
|---|---|
| Hero headline | 56px / 600 / 1.07 / −0.28px |
| Body | 17px / 400 / 1.47 / −0.374px (17px instead of the usual 16px) |

- Display line-heights run 1.07–1.19 (reported) [a10#23].
- **Observed** on the AirPods Max editorial block [cdzbM0HABqY f066]:
  - Headline line pitch is about 47px against a body line pitch of about 12.6px in a roughly 620px-wide capture. That puts the headline at about 7.5% of viewport width per line, and roughly 5.5–6× the body size if body leading is 1.47.
  - Headline leading is about 1.0× the font size or slightly less.
  - The body column is about 32% of viewport width and starts near the horizontal midpoint.
- **Observed** on the iPad Pro hero (2020) [KZ-eAiBW6gA f015]: product name cap height is about 3.4% of viewport width, and the name is about 2.5× the size of the tagline.

**Native scale (HIG, iOS default, size/leading in pt, reported) [a33#7]**

| Style | Size / leading (pt) |
|---|---|
| Large Title | 34/41 |
| Title 1 | 28/34 |
| Title 2 | 22/28 |
| Title 3 | 20/25 |
| Headline | 17/22 Semibold |
| Body | 17/22 |
| Callout | 16/21 |
| Subhead | 15/20 |
| Footnote | 13/18 |
| Caption 1 | 12/16 |
| Caption 2 | 11/13 |

- Body is 17pt and the minimum is 11pt (reported) [a33#0].
- Never use tight leading on three or more lines [a33#10].

**Tracking**
- Native SF tracking table in 1/1000 em (reported) [a33#11]: 12pt 0, 13pt −6, 15pt −16, 17pt −26 (−0.43pt), 20pt −23, 22pt −12, 24pt +3, 28pt +14, 34pt +12, 48pt +8, 60pt +4, 80pt and above 0.
- Web practice differs: display text gets negative tracking (−0.28px at 56px), and nothing at 12px or below (reported) [a10#14].
- Small text is tracked open and large text tighter, which keeps optical spacing even (reported: 18pt Text at +19 and 100pt Display at +7) [a38#3].

**Typographic devices (observed)**
- **Two-tone headline:** a grey lead-in naming the feature over a bright statement ("Pro camera system / We've three-upped ourselves.") [KZ-eAiBW6gA f034]. Also "Looks tough. Because it is." set in grey over two lines [r8QkBKpSj0o f049].
- **Product-colour word:** one word in the product's colour ("You do **orange**.") [cdzbM0HABqY f037].
- **Ghosted wordmark:** a huge low-contrast "iPad Pro" behind the product, with a gradient-filled key line, "Your next computer" [KZ-eAiBW6gA f063].
- **Bold lead-in in captions:** "The **canopy** spanning…" [cdzbM0HABqY f065].
- **Small eyebrows:** "Design", "Performance", an orange "New" [cdzbM0HABqY f066; KZ-eAiBW6gA f066/f017].
- **Tabular figures** for prices and specs [a10#33]. Use tabular figures for animated counters [a38#12].

## 3) Colour, background and material

**Canvases**
- Pages use **white**, light grey **#f5f5f7** ("Parchment") or **pure black**, and nothing else (reported) [a10#3; 5j4HmfgPCDI].
- Observed values:
  - White pages measure 254–255 [cdzbM0HABqY f012/f066; KZ-eAiBW6gA f042; r8QkBKpSj0o f052].
  - The 2020 iPad category page is about #F6F6F6 [KZ-eAiBW6gA f015].
  - The 2014 Watch page is about #F0F0F0 [7PeSluyP8FE f058].
  - Pro pages are exactly #000 [KZ-eAiBW6gA f030/f056].
- "Pro" pages (iPhone 11 Pro, AirPods Pro) get black, cinematic treatment. Mainstream pages stay white or grey [KZ-eAiBW6gA].
- Adjacent dark tiles step through #272729, #2a2a2c and #252527 (reported) [a10#21]. In the iPhone 11 Pro capture, #000 blocks sit inside a surround of about #111 (observed; this could be capture letterboxing) [KZ-eAiBW6gA f030/f032].

**Ink**
- Text is **#1d1d1f**, not #000. Secondary text is #333 / #7a7a7a. Pure black is kept for the global nav, video backgrounds and photo overlays (reported) [a10#18].

**One accent**
- Blue is used only for Buy pills, links and focus (reported) [a10#11/#13]:
  - #0066cc on light surfaces
  - #2997ff for links on dark surfaces
  - #0071e3 for focus rings
- This is confirmed visually across eras [KZ-eAiBW6gA; 7PeSluyP8FE; cdzbM0HABqY].
- "#007AFF" is a community-measured value, not an official Apple one [a44#8].

**Colour comes from the product, not the page**
- Fruit-coloured iMacs, iPhone cases arranged in a colour wheel, vivid art on device screens, lifestyle photos [7PeSluyP8FE f001/f046; KZ-eAiBW6gA f062–071; cdzbM0HABqY f068; xrhNIU-BLZw].
- Colour is tiered by price: premium products get darker, muted tones and entry products get lighter, saturated ones (reported) [7ooF1zNWsrQ].

**No decorative gradients and almost no shadows**
- The extracted web design system has zero gradient tokens; atmosphere comes from photography (reported) [a10#16].
- There is exactly one shadow, `rgba(0,0,0,0.22) 3px 5px 30px`, and it is used only on product renders. Cards get a 1px `rgba(0,0,0,0.08)` hairline instead (reported) [a10#19].

**Radius grammar (reported) [a10#24]**
- 0px for full-bleed tiles, 8px for compact buttons, 11px, 18px for utility cards, and 9999px pills. The pill is reserved for actions.
- Apple uses continuous-curvature "squircle" corners [doiaMtD80p8 f041].

**Material**
- The sticky sub-nav is translucent with backdrop blur instead of a shadow [a10#37].
- On the OS side, Liquid Glass (2025) belongs only to a floating functional layer of controls, never to the content layer [a34#0–1; a35#1].
- The clear Liquid Glass variant over bright media gets a 35% dark dimming layer (reported) [a34#7].
- Liquid Glass is a translucent material that "reflects and refracts its surroundings", takes its tint from the content underneath, and has corner radii concentric with the hardware [a35#0/#3/#4].

## 4) Product imagery and photography

**Fewer images, all extremely high quality, never stock**
- Every image is a commissioned shoot of Apple's own product [KZ-eAiBW6gA].
- Apple's design book used 450+ photos by one photographer, Andrew Zuckerman. Earlier products were re-shot when later shots came out better, to keep the set perfectly consistent (reported) [a42#3/#4]. The style is "analytical and spare," with no personal voice [a43#1].

**Hero cutouts**
- The product is isolated on a seamless white or very light grey background with a soft contact shadow, in a three-quarter view, with generous empty space [xrhNIU-BLZw; 7ooF1zNWsrQ].
- The page background matches the background baked into the render, so the product appears to float with no visible image edges [a40#6].

**Graphic arrangements**
- Colourways arranged in rows, fans or radial wheels: five iMacs in a row, cases in a colour wheel, AirPods in a ring, iPad Air variants fanned on black [7PeSluyP8FE f001/f046; r8QkBKpSj0o f034].

**Bleed and scale**
- Devices bleed off the viewport edge [KZ-eAiBW6gA f015, observed]. A cropped portion of the product can fill most of the frame [KZ-eAiBW6gA f031–036].

**Pro and flagship lighting**
- Low-key shots on pure black with sharp specular highlights tracing the edges [xrhNIU-BLZw f065/f069; KZ-eAiBW6gA f054/f060].

**Materials up close**
- Full-bleed greyscale macro video of knit mesh and stainless steel [cdzbM0HABqY f046–051].
- Extreme crops of edges, ports and crowns [xrhNIU-BLZw].

**Exploded and component views**
- Parts float apart in parallel planes and then settle into the enclosure [xrhNIU-BLZw f031/f038–042; cdzbM0HABqY f052–055].

**Photos that prove a feature**
- A full-bleed red car that shrinks into an iPhone frame with a "1.00" zoom badge [KZ-eAiBW6gA f040–041].
- An ultra-wide low-angle group shot, a dim room for Night mode, and a skateboarder for Portrait [KZ-eAiBW6gA f042–048].
- A hand holding an iPad edge-on to show "Thin, light, and durable" [KZ-eAiBW6gA f026].
- Moody black-and-white climber and diver photos for Watch Ultra [r8QkBKpSj0o f048–051].

**Screens carry colour**
- Device screens show vivid, content-rich third-party art against a neutral page [KZ-eAiBW6gA f062–071].

**Text on images**
- Short, centred white headlines sit directly on full-bleed images [KZ-eAiBW6gA f045/f064/f066; cdzbM0HABqY f046].
- Add a subtle gradient or blur behind text for legibility [dGcqqA3Sl-o#31].

## 5) Motion and scroll storytelling

**Scroll-controlled product film**
- Background video, product shots, titles and paragraphs appear and disappear as you scroll. The visitor controls the pace [KZ-eAiBW6gA].
- Observed: "A customizable fit for all-day comfort." fades in centred on black, then out [KZ-eAiBW6gA f055–057]. Headlines swap over a pinned texture video [cdzbM0HABqY f046–051].

**Product moves to reveal one attribute**
- The iMac rotates to its 11.5 mm profile [cdzbM0HABqY f011–014].
- AirPods Max splits into its parts [cdzbM0HABqY f052–055].
- A full-bleed photo shrinks into a device frame [KZ-eAiBW6gA f040–041].

**Rhythm**
- Video or movement comes right after a text-heavy stretch, and motion alternates with static images. Motion is not continuous. Every motion element needs a job [cdzbM0HABqY].

**How it is built**
- The AirPods Pro hero used a 148-frame JPG sequence drawn to a fixed, centred `<canvas>`. The frame index is `floor(scrollFraction × frameCount)` and frames are drawn through `requestAnimationFrame` (reported) [a40#0–2].
- On slow 3G the page loaded a single fallback image: 8 of 111 requests, 347KB of 2.6MB (reported) [a40#10].
- One article says Apple later moved to scrubbing compressed video, with assets "up to 80% smaller" (reported) [a41#2]. Commenters on the tutorial found MP4 scrubbing laggy (reported) [a40#15].
- Lightweight "content motion" (text fades, pins, slides using CSS transform and opacity) is kept separate from heavy "graphical motion" (sequences, 3D, video) [a41#1/#5/#22].

**Feel**
- Subtle, smooth and quick, never flashy [5j4HmfgPCDI]. Physics-based, interruptible, with spatial transitions instead of hard cuts [doiaMtD80p8; a19#2/#12].
- Buttons scale to 0.95 on press (reported) [a10#25].
- An easing value of `0.00, 0.88, 0.04, 1.00` and masked slide-up / word-by-word reveals come from an "Apple-style" After Effects tutorial. They are not measured from apple.com (reported) [5j4HmfgPCDI].

**Restraint**
- No decorative motion: delight is not "adding confetti" [Xa9L2yx_QH8]. NN/g criticised shimmer and wiggle in iOS 26 [a37#4].
- Never trade INP or LCP for a "premium" look [a41#15].

## 6) Copy voice and headline patterns

**Rules**
- Only a few words on screen at once: one short headline and at most a brief paragraph per view [KZ-eAiBW6gA]. The design book was "a book with very few words" [a42#7; a43#0].
- Headlines are sentence case and end with a full stop, including fragments [cdzbM0HABqY; 7PeSluyP8FE; a10#35].

**Patterns, with examples**

| Pattern | Examples |
|---|---|
| Fragment benefit, with a period | "Symphonic boom." · "High-fidelity audio." · "Portrait-perfect." [cdzbM0HABqY; KZ-eAiBW6gA] |
| Rhythmic triplets or two-beat lines | "Light. Bright. Full of might." [r8QkBKpSj0o] · "Small in size. Big on capability." [KZ-eAiBW6gA] · "Pro. Go. Whoa." · "Chic. Not geek." · "Thinner. Lighter. Faster." [7PeSluyP8FE] |
| Setup, then payoff or wit | "Only 11.5 mm. Now that's thin." [cdzbM0HABqY] · "Try to find a more advanced mobile display. We'll wait." · "Faster than you can say PC." · "Not enough light? No problem." · "OK, everyone, scooch out a bit." [KZ-eAiBW6gA] · "Looks tough. Because it is." [r8QkBKpSj0o] |
| Category reframe | "Your next computer is not a computer." [KZ-eAiBW6gA] · "Apple reinvents the phone." [7PeSluyP8FE] |
| Wordplay on the brand | "We've three-upped ourselves." · "The all-in-one for all." [KZ-eAiBW6gA] · "The best for the brightest." [7PeSluyP8FE] |
| Direct address or imperative | "Seven vibrant colors. You do orange." [cdzbM0HABqY] · "Adventure awaits." [r8QkBKpSj0o] · "Calling all pros." · "Wrap up your gift list." [7PeSluyP8FE] |
| Narrative arrival | "And then there was Pro." [KZ-eAiBW6gA] · "The Watch is here." · "iPhone / This is 7." [7PeSluyP8FE] |
| Editorial statement | "A radically original composition." [cdzbM0HABqY] |

**Supporting copy**
- Feature callouts open with the bolded part name, then a single plain sentence [cdzbM0HABqY f065/f068].
- Price lines are exact and include monthly terms: "From $1299 or $108.25/mo. for 12 mo." [cdzbM0HABqY]; "From $29.95/mo. or $719 with trade-in" [KZ-eAiBW6gA].
- CTAs are a single blue "Buy" pill plus text links ending in ">", such as "Learn more >", "View the World Gallery >" and "Watch the film" [7PeSluyP8FE; KZ-eAiBW6gA].
- Copy plus motion walks through the product's value one component at a time, a "story of value" [cdzbM0HABqY].

## 7) Interface principles from the HIG and WWDC talks

**Earlier HIG trio: Clarity, Deference, Depth** [a44#15–17; pY52H5gKhGg]
- Clarity: everything is legible and precise.
- Deference: chrome recedes so content leads, and whitespace does the work of hierarchy [a44#14].
- Depth: layering and motion convey structure.

**2025 HIG trio (Liquid Glass era): Hierarchy, Harmony, Consistency** [a36; doiaMtD80p8 f047]
- **Hierarchy:** prioritise the most important content. Controls sit above it. Build hierarchy with spacing, size and component behaviour, not just colour or effects [a36#0–1].
- **Harmony:** shapes are concentric with the hardware's rounded corners [a36#5; a35#3].
- **Consistency:** follow platform conventions while adapting to each window size. "Think in systems, not screens" [a36#9/#12].
- From the Newsroom: chrome "gives way to content." Tab bars shrink as you scroll down and expand again as you scroll up. Familiar mental models are kept [a35#2/#8/#12].

**WWDC25 "Design foundations"** [dGcqqA3Sl-o]
- Every screen should answer three questions: **Where am I? What can I do? Where can I go?**
- Do an information-architecture pass first: list everything, then remove, rename and group. Use progressive disclosure.
- Squint at the screen to test hierarchy, and give it one visual anchor that is larger or higher in contrast.
- Use system text styles, not eyeballed sizes. Use semantic colours (label, secondarySystemBackground) that adapt to light, dark and increased contrast. Use the accent colour sparingly.
- Know "when to lean on the system, and where to add personality."

**WWDC26 "Principles of great design" (eight principles)** [Xa9L2yx_QH8]
- **Purpose, Agency, Responsibility, Familiarity, Flexibility, Simplicity, Craft, Delight.**
- "Design is making something with intention." Every feature costs people time, attention and trust.
- "When we say simple, we don't mean minimal."
- Every element earns its place. Turn complex data into graphics.
- Craft comes from materials: fonts that look good on every device, colours that adapt between light and dark, clear icons, and responsive animation.
- Delight is the *sum* of the other principles, not confetti added at the end.
- The principles trade off against each other; no formula combines them.

**Typography HIG** [a33]
- Minimise the number of typefaces.
- Build hierarchy with weight, size and colour.
- Use emphasized (bold) variants instead of adding new sizes.
- Lay out for every Dynamic Type size.

**Craft ethos behind the pages**
- People sense care they cannot put into words, so Apple finishes the parts nobody sees [xrhNIU-BLZw; a43#11].
- Apple won't ship something that is merely different rather than better [xrhNIU-BLZw].
- Jobs's "staying beginners": see the product through a new customer's eyes, every day [9uOMectkCCs].
- Remove everything, then add back only what is needed [doiaMtD80p8].
- The website has always followed the product's materials and OS look of its era (Aqua, then aluminum, then glass, then flat) [7PeSluyP8FE].

## 8) What NOT to copy (or copy only with care)

**Scroll-scrubbed product films and 3D build-up sequences**
- These need pre-rendered CGI of a physical product and a lot of engineering.
- The tutorial's naive reproduction with several sequences on one page cost 1,609 requests, 55.8MB and a 30s load (reported) [a40#9]. Apple ships device-sized sequences and a fallback for slow connections [a40#10/#11].
- Most copies "chase the look, not the logic" [a41#11]. Copy the pacing and the one-idea-per-viewport structure, not the asset pipeline.

**Bespoke photography at Apple's level**
- One photographer, re-shoots for consistency, macro material films, exploded renders and manufacturing footage [a42#3/#4; xrhNIU-BLZw; cdzbM0HABqY]. Without that, a stark white page shows the weakness of the images.
- Never fill the gap with stock photos [KZ-eAiBW6gA].

**"Slow down" before the price**
- Apple consciously delays content and price for an intro "experience" [cdzbM0HABqY].
- *My inference:* this works because visitors already want the product. A lesser-known brand risks losing visitors who came for facts.

**Witty fragment headlines and pure-black "Pro" pages**
- These lean on brand familiarity and on product colourways that do the colour work [KZ-eAiBW6gA; 7ooF1zNWsrQ].
- *My inference:* without a recognisable product, the one-word headlines may read as vague.

**SF Pro itself**
- Do not embed or ship the system fonts; reference them through the system [a33#16].
- On the web, use the `system-ui` stack or a re-tuned Inter [a10#31]. Even Malewicz had to substitute a font in his redesign [r8QkBKpSj0o].

**Liquid Glass and glassmorphism as decoration**
- The HIG limits glass to a few functional controls [a34#3].
- NN/g documents contrast, crowding and moving-navigation problems in iOS 26 [a37].
- Frosted "glassmorphism" cards used as decoration are a style label and an AI tell [A_h4A8caCTM#19].
- After Effects tropes from "Apple-style" tutorials (bevel/emboss, glassmorphism panels) are not apple.com patterns [5j4HmfgPCDI].

**Token values treated as official**
- #007AFF, #0071e3 and similar values are community-measured approximations [a44#8; a10].

**Template tells that break the effect**
- Display weight of 700 or more [a10#15].
- Mesh, aurora or glow gradients [a10#16].
- Shadows on cards or buttons [a10#19].
- Bento grids with empty cells [a05#22].

**Apple's own misses**
- The "Saved products" utility page: a centred title, an orphaned Edit link, and one saved item hidden behind an extra click [r8QkBKpSj0o].
- Copy the marketing-page discipline, not every Apple screen.
